# Prop tools: page split, accounts manager, sizing suggester (plan and tracker)

This file tracks every feature from the 2026-09-25 request until it is built and verified. Nothing gets dropped. An item leaves `todo` for exactly one of two final statuses:
- `done`: implemented RED then GREEN, reviewed, wave checks green, and confirmed by the final verification pass.
- `blocked`: with the reason recorded.

- **Source record:** [`DECISIONS.md`](DECISIONS.md). It holds the request verbatim, the user decisions P1 to P4, and the scope directive ("build as much features ... track it properly").
- **Work packages:** [`packages.md`](packages.md). Each `## PT-xx:` section holds steps, file ownership, RED-first tests and acceptance. The wave runner reads a section by that heading.
- **Process:** each package runs ECC orch-add-feature:
  1. `ecc:tdd-guide` writes failing tests first, confirms RED, then implements to GREEN.
  2. The package's reviewers review (see "Review plan").
  3. Fix loop, then re-review.
  4. Wave checks.
  GATE 2 (commit) is skipped: the user does all commits.
- **Sibling workstream:** the prop-engine audit (`.claude/plans/prop-engine-audit-2026-09-23/PLAN.md`). Wave 18 (WP17d, WP23) is DONE. Wave 19 runs WP24, the last audit package. After it the audit runs its "After all packages" work: the final santa pass over every item with sha256 file fingerprints (fixed items are verified incrementally and re-verified only if their fingerprinted files change), the firm notes audit, engine-results reruns, the T4 fee sweep and `bun run check`.
  - WP24 owns: `core/DayPolicy.ts`, `simulator/fundedPhase.ts`, `core/FundedStateValue.ts`, `core/FundedDpModelGaps.ts`, `core/EvalStateValue.ts`, `core/Plan.ts` (`tierProfitContext`), `core/DailyLossLimit.ts`, `core/TierBasis.ts`, `simulator/index.ts`, `firms/index.ts`, `src/lib/prop-calculator/index.ts`, `firms/alphafutures/AlphaFutures.ts`, `firms/e8futures/E8Futures.ts`, `firms/lucid/LucidTrading.ts` (notes), `src/cli/commands/prop/sim/command.ts`, `src/cli/commands/prop/optimize/dp/command.ts`, `src/app/(app)/prop-calculator/_components/labMath.ts`, and their tests, including the three label tests under `tests/unit/app/prop-calculator` that PT-10, PT-11 and PT-17 run as guards. WP24 also adds `fundedResetsUsed` to `FundedCycleSnapshot` (N-34) and makes the funded DP pick its policy by reset layer.
  - WP23 (done) owned `src/app/(app)/prop-calculator/**` (CalculatorShell, useCalculator, TradingInputs, PortfolioPanel, LadderLabPanel and the R1-21 cash-flow wiring), `core/AccountState.ts`, `LivePlan.ts`, `TierBasis.ts`, `PeakRatchet.ts`, the simulator day/eval/funded/types files and `optimize/funded/command.ts`. Those web files carry audit items whose santa fingerprints lane W will change.
  - Lane 0 (waves 1 and 2) touches none of the audit-owned files. Lane W waits for gate G1. Lane E (engine and firm files) waits for gate G2. See "Schedule".

Revision 2 (2026-09-25) folded in the architecture, trader and React reviews of revision 1. Revision 3 (2026-09-25) folds in the second critique of revision 2 (36 findings: 14 HIGH, 22 MEDIUM). "Review findings resolution (revision 2)" and "Review findings resolution (revision 3)" map every finding to the decision, item or package that resolves it. F-item and PT-package numbers are stable; revision 3 adds F-148 to F-156 and PT-47 to PT-49.

## Goal

Three deliverables, in dependency order:

- **(A) Page split.** Split the 427-line `/prop-calculator` page (`CalculatorShell.tsx`, 15 stacked sections, about 9 simulation batches per keystroke) into a hub with child tool pages. Every existing panel moves with no behaviour change, and old share links (query and section fragment) keep working.
- **(B) Accounts manager.** A DB-backed prop accounts manager behind sign-in:
  - accounts, balance snapshots, payouts, fees, lifecycle events, copy groups and a personal rulebook;
  - an overview built from every metric the code and the rules support, led by spend, payouts received and realized net.
- **(C) Per-account sizing suggester.**
  - The headline is the user's documented rule (prop-firm-trading skill, Hard Rules 1 to 8).
  - The engine optimum sits alongside it, with a typed reason for every difference.
  - Every number carries its source rule, its assumptions and its start basis.
  - Every engine run uses the user's retained cushion, the effective payout request (the larger of the rulebook size and the plan's minimum) under the "wait for the full request" policy, and the verified live-trigger payout cap, never the plan's own defaults. Where an engine source cannot honor that policy, the row says so with a typed reason.

## Scope: milestones (v1) and deferred

| Milestone | Lane and waves | What the user gets |
|---|---|---|
| M0 foundations | Lane 0, waves 1-2 (start now) | Tables and migration, userId-scoped tRPC routers with abuse limits, documented eval/funded/live rules, ledger metrics, alert framework, firm-policy live research. No UI yet. |
| M1 split | Lane W, W3-W5 (after G1) | Hub plus 8 child pages carrying today's panels, provider state that stays in step with the URL (path, query and fragment), legacy redirects, per-page SEO, headings and landmarks, code splitting, a typed result cache. |
| M2 manager | Lane W W4-W6, lane J | Accounts area (sign-in), add/edit form, list, CSV import/export, rulebook page, journal edge, saved-scenario sync, then overview, detail, copy groups, public position-size and funded-optimizer tools, CLI `prop advise`. |
| M3 suggester | Lane E E1-E7 (after G2), lane J J1-J4 | State reconstruction, payout gates and the engine payout request policy, documented payout rule on the post-payout floor, documented-policy simulation, SizingAdvisor per stage, simulate standard errors, advice card with the documented rule first and the engine optima (fresh and from-state) alongside, weekly review, payout advice, plausibility at every entry point. |
| M4 extended | Lane E E3-E4, lane J J2-J5 | Firm account-policy model (pooled caps, live triggers, live exclusivity and cooldowns, inactivity, conduct patterns), plan-rule fingerprints, DP payout policy in the solver, DP advice at the exact state (CLI solve, stored, validated only from a fresh gate run), projections, next-slot allocation with payout-policy sensitivity, hub extras, cross-plan copy-group simulation. |
| M5 wrap-up | Final | Integration, security and DB review sweep, skill doc update, per-item verification. |

"Deferred (blocked)" below lists what is genuinely blocked, and on what.

## Decisions (planner, 2026-09-25; binding unless the user changes them)

| # | Topic | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| PD-1 | Access | `protectedProcedure` (any signed-in user; `src/server/api/trpc.ts:79-90`), per P1 "sign-in required". Not `rootProcedure` (`trpc.ts:103-112`). The calculator tools stay public. Open signup exists, so PD-36 adds abuse limits. U1 lets the user switch to root-only. |
| PD-2 | Naming | Every artifact of the new manager is named "Accounts": `routes.propCalculator.accounts`, router key `propAccounts`, lib `~/lib/prop-accounts`, tables `sadranl_prop_*`. The existing what-if `PortfolioPanel` becomes the "Multi-firm planner" at `/prop-calculator/planner`, URL key `pf` unchanged. "Portfolio" is avoided because `routes.portfolio` is the CV page (`routes.ts:41`). |
| PD-3 | Money | Stored as integer cents (`*_cents integer`), typed by a branded `UsdCents` with Zod bounds. Totals are integer sums. Conversion to the engine's `Dollars` happens only at the engine boundary. Form input is parsed from the decimal string. Not the accounting `Eur` helper: it is EUR-only and accounting-scoped (`money.ts:19-26`). |
| PD-4 | Plan identity | Stored as `firmId`, `planSerial` (`serializePlanId`, `PlanId.ts:138-141`), `optIns` (`PlanOptIns`) and `accountSize`. The PlanKey refine rejects a firm or size that disagrees with the serial. It resolves through a new `TradingFirm.findPlanBySerial`, which replaces 4 hand-written scans (`urlState.ts:82,170`, `FirmPlanPicker.tsx:79`, `PortfolioPanel.tsx:667`). An unresolvable plan is shown read-only with a warning and never throws. |
| PD-5 | Rule options | "Less DLL" and similar are plan variants plus the opt-ins the plan offers. Offered opt-ins come from one predicate set: `takesFundedReset` only when `plan.fundedReset !== null`, `takesOneTimeEarlyWithdrawal` only when `plan.oneTimeEarlyWithdrawal !== null`. The engine's `withPlanOptIns` silently ignores an unoffered opt-in (`FundedReset.ts:74-78`, `FundedPayoutCycle.ts:332-339`), so the schema rejects explicitly. Personal stricter limits are stored separately (PD-37). |
| PD-6 | Account size | A stored column. Only modeled sizes are selectable: 50K today (`PlanId.ts:123-132`). Other sizes are shown disabled as "not modeled". |
| PD-7 | Route tree and landmarks | The hub `/prop-calculator` is public. No page in this app is static: the root layout awaits `getServerSession()` (`src/app/layout.tsx:52`), so every route renders dynamically; the plan makes no "static" claim. A route group `(tools)` has a server `layout.tsx` (lifting precedent) that renders the client `CalculatorProvider`, the subnav (portaled into `#navbar-subnav-slot`) and `<main className="app-prop-calculator container pt-spacing pb-24">` around `children`. Tool pages render no `<main>`. Each tool page starts with `ToolPageHeading`, which renders the `<h1>` (catalog label) followed by `<CalculatorToolbar/>` (share link and saved scenarios, shown only when the catalog entry has `sharesState`), so the toolbar sits inside `<main>` after the `<h1>`. Every section of a tool page is wrapped in `<section id="<legacy id>" aria-labelledby>` with an `<h2>` (sr-only on single-panel pages where a visible `<h2>` would repeat the `<h1>`), so the outline is h1, h2, then the panels' existing h3/h4. Child pages: simulator, analysis, sizing, compare, cash-flow, ladder-lab, strategy-lab, planner; new tools: position-size, funded-optimizer, live, rules, payout-planner. `accounts/**` sits outside the group, so the auth pages never run the calculator. |
| PD-8 | URL and state | The provider state is the source of truth while the tools layout is mounted; the URL mirrors it. (a) Initial state: the provider initializes its reducer lazily from `useSearchParams()` through `decodeState`, on the server and the client alike (routes are dynamic, so the query is available during SSR), so the first render already holds a shared link's state and each panel computes once. The legacy hash is read on the client only, in a mount effect, before the first URL write. (b) Writer: runs on `[state, pathname, searchParams]` and only on pages whose catalog entry has `usesCalculatorInputs`. It compares the canonical form of the current search (`searchParams.toString()`) with the canonical form of `encodeState(state)` (`new URLSearchParams(encodeState(state)).toString()`), and replaces the entry only when they differ, keeping the current `window.location.hash`. That covers clicking the active subnav item, Back and Forward between entries with the same path and different queries, and never loops on re-encoding. (c) Share link: `ShareLinkButton` builds `origin + pathname + '?' + encodeState(state)` from the provider, never `window.location.href`. (d) Subnav: hrefs are the base route plus the debounced encoded query (the same debounce as the simulation, so no per-keystroke churn) with `prefetch={false}`, so middle-click, Cmd-click and "copy link" open the tool with the current inputs. On same-layout navigation the provider ignores the incoming query (state wins) and the writer re-syncs. (e) Links inside `(tools)` that should change inputs (for example "open in simulator") dispatch provider actions, then `router.push` the target path. Only links that cross into `(tools)` from outside (hub, accounts, "simulate this account", shared links) are hydrated from their query, through (a). `encodeState`/`decodeState` are unchanged (`urlState.ts:69-301`), so saved scenarios stay valid. |
| PD-9 | Legacy links | Query layer: `proxy.ts` redirects (307) `/prop-calculator?firm=...` to `/prop-calculator/simulator` using the raw `request.nextUrl.search`, so the query is byte-identical (a page-level `redirect()` would re-serialize a parsed `searchParams` object, which can reorder or re-encode). Fragment layer: old links almost always carry a section hash (SectionNav rewrites `#<section>` on scroll, `SectionNav.tsx:78,118`). The provider's mount step and the hub map a legacy hash (typed `LegacySection` enum, one table) to `<route>#<id>`, keeping the id; the page shells keep every legacy section id (with `scroll-mt-26`), and the mapper scrolls to the id after the target section mounts (heavy panels load after hydration through `next/dynamic`). Wired only when the shell retires (PT-43). |
| PD-10 | Base simulation | Computed once in the provider, only after mount (never during SSR, which today runs a 2,000-trial `simulate` per request, `useCalculator.ts:221`), only while a page that reads `result` is mounted (simulator, analysis), keyed by the debounced inputs, and shared by those two pages. Because the reducer is initialized from the query (PD-8 a), the first run already uses a shared link's inputs; it also waits for the mount-time legacy-hash decision, so defaults are never computed first. `useBaseSimulation` returns `{ result: SimOutputs \| null, isPending }`; until `result` is non-null, the page views and the transitional CalculatorShell render a `Skeleton` for every result-consuming section, and panel props stay unchanged. `pinScenario` takes the result as an argument. |
| PD-11 | Inputs off the simulator page | One provider-connected `CalculatorInputsForm` renders FirmPlanPicker, PlanStatsBadges and TradingInputs from context (including the new `fundedHorizonDays` control); SimulatorView and `EditInputsDialog` both render it, so the ~60-prop wiring exists once. Non-simulator pages whose catalog entry has `usesCalculatorInputs` show a compact inputs summary plus the "Edit inputs" `Dialog`. DialogContent gets `max-h-[85dvh] overflow-y-auto`, a `DialogTitle` and a `DialogDescription`; focus returns to the trigger on close. |
| PD-12 | Panel-local state | Lifted into the provider: `pinned`, `chartType`, and a ladder slot that stores the last result together with the full `LadderSearchInputs` it was scored under (including `stopPoints`) and the lab's display instrument. Applicability is decided by one key function, `ladderInputsKey(inputs)` (built on the `simInputsCacheKey` conventions), never a hand-enumerated list. Restoring the slot restores the form fields and the display instrument; Apply uses the stored stop rule. A slot whose key differs from the current inputs is shown with an "inputs changed since this run" notice and Apply disabled. The slot is written only on completion, cancel or unmount, never on each progress update. Other panel-local state resets on navigation, as it does on reload today. |
| PD-13 | Suggester presentation (P2) | The headline is the documented rule from the user's rulebook, labeled "your documented rule" while the rulebook equals the skill defaults and "your custom rule (differs from Hard Rule N)" otherwise. Engine optima are shown alongside, each with `AdviceSource`, objective, start basis, trials and (where the engine provides it) standard error. Differences are explained by typed `DifferenceReason`s whose text is built only from typed numeric fields of the advice inputs. |
| PD-14 | Documented eval rule | Default mode is the skill's "General derivation for any plan" ladder (`EvalLadderRule` with `LadderFractionSource.GeneralDerivation`, labeled with that section anchor): rung 1 = 20% of the day-start cushion, each next rung 1.5x the previous (rounded down to the rounding step), the final rung = whatever cushion remains, at most `tradesPerDayMax` rungs, sum = cushion exactly; daily cap = consistency pct x profit target on consistency plans. Opt-in modes: the MFF Rapid EOD 50K search fractions [0.2, 0.3, 0.4, 0.1] (`LadderFractionSource.MffRapidEodSearch`, labeled "from the MFF Rapid EOD 50K search, extrapolated to this plan"), and Hard Rules 3-4 max-risk (`EvalMaxRiskRule`: risk = min(cushion, day-start DLL room, consistency daily cap / 2, remaining profit to target / rr), daily cap = 2 x risk, at most 4 trades). In every mode: each trade k risks at most the affordable risk after the running loss (`cushion - running loss`, and the day-start DLL room minus the running loss); the running loss never exceeds min(cushion, day-start DLL room, personal DLL); each rung is capped at (remaining profit to target + running loss) / rr so a win never overshoots the target; each green-day outcome is capped by the consistency daily cap. U4 only picks the default mode; all modes are built and rulebook-editable. |
| PD-15 | State reconstruction (P4) | Required: balance, highest EOD balance, stage, payouts taken, trading days. Optional extras have disclosed defaults. Intraday-trailing plans require the highest intraday balance or the dashboard floor. The dashboard convention (nominal vs $0-based) is a per-account input defaulting to nominal, with plausibility checks that fail loud on a likely convention mix-up (F-140) at every entry point. The peak is replayed through the plan's own `DrawdownStrategy`. For lock-effect plans the input "floor at last payout" (or the dashboard floor) removes the peak-order ambiguity; without it the conservative order (higher floor) is assumed and disclosed. Calendar day gates are computed from real dates (`first_funded_trade_on`, `last_payout_on`), not the engine's 5/7 session approximation. `fundedResetsUsed` is derived from the account's `FundedReset` events (override allowed in `AccountSnapshotInput` and the CLI). `cumulativePayout` is trader-received net, matching `FundedCycleTracker.cumulativePayout` (`FundedPayoutCycle.ts:152`): the snapshot value when given, else the sum of Paid payouts' `net_cents` (gross with a `GrossOnlyPayouts` assumption where net is missing). Live reconstruction follows PD-41. |
| PD-16 | DP | Never per request, never in the browser. DP code lives in a Node-only subpath `~/lib/prop-calculator/advisor/dp` that `advisor/index.ts` never re-exports, imported only from `src/cli/**`. `bun run cli prop advise --dp` solves once per distinct full config (every parameter the solver actually honors is hashed into `config_key`; payout request size and policy enter the key only because PT-47 threads them into the solve), computes eligibility and gaps in the CLI, evaluates at each account's rebuilt state, and can `--store` samples plus eligibility for the web. A per-run plan cap and time budget bound `--all`. If PT-47 is blocked, every DP row carries the typed gap `PayoutSizeNotModeled` and the reason `DpPayoutPolicyMismatch` ("DP assumes maximum withdrawal"), and payout size is dropped from `config_key`. |
| PD-17 | Browser compute | Ladder, funded sweep, overview expected-net, projections, next-slot and advisor optima run in Web Workers with typed enum message protocols (as `ladderWorkerMessages.ts`), cached per full request key (PD-26), so accounts on the same plan and policy share fresh-start results. Messages carry only serializable data: a documented policy travels as a `DocumentedPolicySpec` (PD-39) and the worker builds the `computeRisk` closure itself. |
| PD-18 | Firm policy data | Pooled caps, live triggers, live exclusivity and cooldowns, inactivity, conduct triggers and per-plan live applicability enter code only after live help-center verification (PT-04 research, then PT-12 and PT-35). 403 sites (Apex, Tradeify help, E8 help, every lucidtrading.com page) need pages pasted by the user. |
| PD-19 | Router boundaries | Every procedure gets `.input()` and `.output()`. Row outputs use drizzle-zod `createSelectSchema` with column overrides for every enum and jsonb column (precedent `src/lib/schemas/trading.ts:184-196`), and a type test asserts `z.infer` of each output schema is assignable to the table's `$inferSelect`. Every jsonb column is parsed on read by a schema that defaults missing keys; rulebook parameters carry a `schemaVersion`. No router uses `.output()` today; the older pattern is flagged for cleanup, not blended. |
| PD-20 | Same-owner integrity | Required child references use composite FKs `(account_id, user_id)` onto `prop_account(id, user_id)`. The target is a table-level `unique('prop_account_id_user_id_uq').on(t.id, t.userId)`, which drizzle-kit emits inside `CREATE TABLE`; a unique index would be emitted after the FKs (`drizzle/0001_goofy_sage.sql:26-33`) and the migration would fail. Same for `prop_copy_group`. **Amended 2026-09-25 after the PT-01 review (HIGH):** the nullable references are composite too, so the database itself rejects a link to another user's row: `(replaces_account_id, user_id)` onto `prop_account(id, user_id)`, `(copy_group_id, user_id)` onto `prop_copy_group(id, user_id)`, and `(snapshot_id, account_id, user_id)` onto a table-level unique `prop_account_snapshot(id, account_id, user_id)` (so a decision can only point at a snapshot of its own account). They use `ON DELETE NO ACTION` (MATCH SIMPLE: a null reference is not checked), because `SET NULL` on a composite key would null `user_id`; the routers clear the referencing column in the same transaction before `account.remove`, `copyGroup.remove` and `snapshot.remove`, and whole-user or whole-account cascades delete both sides in one statement. The router ownership checks stay (defense in depth). Every query still carries `userId`. |
| PD-21 | Sequencing | Lane 0 (waves 1-2) starts now, touching no audit-owned file and not `core/index.ts`. Lane W (web split, accounts CRUD UI) waits for G1: WP24 implemented and re-reviewed, and the audit's santa pass has recorded fingerprints for every audit item whose files lie in `src/app/(app)/prop-calculator/**` or `src/lib/site/**`. Lane E (every package that edits `src/lib/prop-calculator/**` engine or firm files, or `src/cli/commands/prop/**`) waits for G2: the audit's final santa pass over every item, its fingerprint re-check and its `bun run check`, with outcomes recorded; G2 excludes the paste-blocked parked firm-data steps and T4 fee-sweep rows blocked on 403 pages, which continue in parallel (lane E packages that edit a firm file re-check the audit tracker for them first). Lane J joins after lane E. Neither workstream runs repo-wide `bun run check`, `bun run verify`, `bun run lint` or `format:write` while the other has files in flight, and no lane 0 package may sit between RED and GREEN while an audit package or audit check is running (U21). Clarified 2026-09-26: U21's intent is that neither workstream runs a repo-wide check (`bun run check`, `verify`, `lint`, `format:write`) or a santa/fingerprint pass while the other has a package between RED and GREEN; packages with disjoint files may run side by side (typed RED stubs keep the typecheck at baseline). PT-01b ran next to audit wave 22 on that basis. |
| PD-22 | Verification | Never `bun run dev`/`build`, no browser automation, no `bun run lint` (it is `eslint . --fix`, `package.json:20`). Use `bun run typecheck` (against a recorded baseline: a wave fails only on errors in files it owns or files importing them), `bunx eslint <files>` without `--fix`, the formatter on owned files only, `bunx vitest run <tests>` from the repo root. RED tests must fail at runtime, not at the type level: a package first adds each new module or method as a typed stub that throws `new Error('not implemented')`, then writes tests against it, so a project-wide typecheck stays at the baseline during RED (tsconfig includes `**/*.ts`, so tests are typechecked). knip is weak evidence: `knip.json:3-10` makes `src/lib/**` and `_components/**` entries, so every new file also gets an explicit "has an importer outside tests" grep and every deleted module a "no importer left" grep, recorded in the wave log. Worker files are reachable only via `new URL(..., import.meta.url)`; PT-10 confirms knip resolves them or adds worker entries. UI logic goes into `.ts` helpers (Vitest includes `tests/unit/**/*.test.ts` only, node environment, `vitest.config.ts:23-25`). |
| PD-23 | Migrations | The package runs `bun run db:generate` and inspects the generated SQL: only `sadranl_prop_*` DDL, and every FK references a constraint created earlier in the same file. Otherwise stop and report. `bun run db:migrate` runs only when the user says so (U3). |
| PD-24 | Schema module graph | `schemas/prop.ts` stays value-free toward `~/lib`, like every existing schema file (type-only imports: `accounting.ts:15-27`, `trading.ts:15-24`, `lifting.ts:16-38`). Enum columns have no DB default (routers always supply the value); jsonb defaults use `sql` literals; `.$type<Enum>()` uses `import type`. This keeps the engine out of `~/server/db` and avoids relying on drizzle-kit resolving `~/` value imports. |
| PD-25 | Contract caps | The documented rules never convert a contract cap to dollars (SKILL.md "Stop distance is NOT fixed": "Never convert a contract cap to dollars via an assumed point stop"). The contract limit is reported as minimum stop points at the suggested risk via the existing `minStopPoints` (`ContractLimits.ts:83`), flagged only when it exceeds a stop the user entered for today. Dollar contract capping appears only in engine-optimum rows, with the engine's instrument and stop shown as assumptions. |
| PD-26 | EnginePolicy in every engine run | Every `EngineOptimumRequest`, worker message, CLI solve and cache key carries one `EnginePolicy`: `minRetainedCushion` (rulebook; at least $2,000 unless `allowBelowHardRule2`, Hard Rule 2); the effective payout request (PD-40) with `PayoutRequestPolicy.FullRequestOnly`; `lifetimePayoutCapOverride` from PT-35's verified live count triggers (applied with `plan.withMaxLifetimePayouts`, `Plan.ts:981`), or none with a `LiveTriggersNotChecked` reason marked optimistic; `intradayPathStepsPerR` (10, the ledger's path-walk granularity, on by default for intraday-trailing plans); the documented funded day policy (risk, TP multiple, trades per day, stop rule, from PT-03); winrate and rr; `rebuyLagDays` (the measured F-76 lag when n > 0, else 0 with an optimistic `AssumedInputs` reason); `fundedHorizonDays`; commission; ladder fractions; the plan serial, opt-ins and plan-rule fingerprint. The engine default `plan.resolveRetainedCushion(undefined)` is never used (TopStep's override is $0, `Plan.ts:900-909`). Live retained cushion = `max(rulebook retained cushion, one full live drawdown (audit D4))`, with a `RetainedCushionBasis` reason. |
| PD-27 | Documented payout rule | Built on the non-mutating `evaluatePayout` and the plan's prospective post-payout floor (`DrawdownStrategy.prospectiveLockThreshold`, `DrawdownStrategy.ts:75-90`, plus `payoutBalanceFloor` and `payoutBuffer`). The request is the effective request (PD-40). A request is allowed only if `balance - request - postPayoutFloor >= retainedCushion` and every firm gate passes (min request, caps, day gate, consistency, pending payout, live trigger once verified). When it is not allowed the answer is "wait: $X more profit (or N qualifying days) to unlock", never a smaller request. When the plan minimum raised the request above the rulebook size, the advice says "firm minimum $M is above your $R". |
| PD-28 | Staleness per stage | Eval and live advice is stale when the latest snapshot predates the last trading session (weekdays; no holiday calendar, disclosed); the headline is replaced by "enter today's balance" and no rung amounts are shown. Funded advice is stale after the rulebook's weekly threshold. The snapshot date is shown on every headline. |
| PD-29 | Instant-funded plans | `AccountLifecycle` is plan-aware: Purchased gives Funded/Active when `plan.isInstantFunded` (`Plan.ts:184`; set in TopStep, Lucid, Tradeify, FundedNext firm files), and stage Eval is rejected for such plans in the form, the router and `createSizingAdvisor` (typed error, never eval advice). |
| PD-30 | Copy groups | Accounts are independent unless placed in a group (SKILL.md "Confirmed assumptions": not copy-traded). A group's members must share one stage; `copyGroup.assign` rejects a mixed-stage group with a typed reason, and an alert fires when a lifecycle event makes a group mixed. Eval groups are sized rung by rung (the minimum per rung with each member's cumulative DLL); funded groups by the minimum flat risk. |
| PD-31 | Characterization baselines | "Unchanged" means unchanged from pins captured from the working tree at the start of the package (after G2), written into the test files, never "from HEAD": the tree holds uncommitted audit work and the user commits WIP snapshots at arbitrary points. |
| PD-32 | Reason text | Reason and headline text is built only from typed numeric fields of the advice inputs. The test injects sentinel values and asserts the text contains exactly those numbers; no assertion about "figures from the skill's tables" (250, 500, 2,000 are also rulebook defaults). |
| PD-33 | Kelly and the sizing invariant | Kelly stays in StrategyAnalysis only. No per-account advice shows it (full Kelly at 40%/1:2 is 0.10 of account size, about $5,000 on 50K, 2.5x the whole cushion). Invariant test on every advice view model and every documented sizing: each rung is at most the `resolveRiskAt` affordable risk after the running loss of the earlier rungs, and the running loss never exceeds min(cushion, day-start DLL room, personal DLL). |
| PD-34 | Standard errors | Only `LadderSearch` returns standard errors today (`LadderSearch.ts:538-621`); `SimOutputs` has none (`simulator/types.ts:277-331`). PT-44 adds per-trial estimates to `simulate` using `stats.ts` (`Estimate`, `meanStandardError`, `binomialStandardError`, `propagatedStandardError`). Until PT-44 lands, simulate-based sources show no SE and never emit `WithinNoise`. |
| PD-35 | Shell retirement | `CalculatorShell.tsx` and `SectionNav.tsx` are deleted, and the hub replaces `page.tsx`, only after all 8 tool pages exist (PT-43, after PT-11 and PT-17). Until then the shell keeps working (traced after every lane W wave). |
| PD-36 | Abuse limits | Per-user row caps (defaults U20: 200 accounts, 20,000 snapshots, 5,000 payouts, 5,000 fees, 50 copy groups, 100 saved scenarios), a mutation rate limit via the existing `isWithinRateLimit` (`src/lib/observability/rate-limit.ts:8`, bucket per router, key = userId), and Zod length and count bounds on every text, array and jsonb field. List procedures that span accounts are bounded (date range and row limit). |
| PD-37 | Personal rules | Personal dollar risk, DLL, trades per day, daily profit cap and retained cushion are applied only as tighter caps (`PersonalCap`). Personal payout request size is an override, not a cap (payout size is not monotonic in safety, SKILL.md payout-size section), shown with a warning taken from the payout-size sweep once PT-32 lands; the effective request is still at least the plan minimum (PD-40). |
| PD-38 | Rulebook integrity | The rulebook stores parameters only for rules that are numbers in the skill. Eval risk is not stored: Hard Rule 3 defines it as "max allowed by the constraints", and the general-derivation ladder stores only its skill numbers (first rung 20%, escalation 1.5). Retained cushion below $2,000 requires an explicit `allowBelowHardRule2` flag and relabels every headline as custom. A `deviatesFrom: RuleSource[]` diff against the skill defaults is computed, shown on the rulebook page and previewed before a trade-checklist import is applied. `RuleSource` cites section anchors ("Hard Rule 5", "Eval ladder", "General derivation for any plan"), never line numbers. |
| PD-39 | Documented policy simulation | Every "under the documented rule" engine number (F-85, F-87, F-96, projections, next slot) runs the documented rule itself. `DocumentedPolicySpec` is serializable (rulebook parameters, plan key, stage, `EnginePolicy`); a pure `toSimInputs(plan, spec)` builds the `SimInputs`, including a `computedDayPolicy` whose `computeRisk` closure applies the documented rule at each trade (state-dependent max-risk or ladder rungs, cumulative DLL, consistency cap, remaining-target cap, the 2 x day-start risk daily cap). Workers receive the spec and call `toSimInputs` themselves. If `day.ts` does not end the day when `computeRisk` returns 0, or the day-start values cannot be recovered from `AccountState`, PT-48 adds a day-start-relative stop kind to `DayPolicy.ts` with pins for the existing kinds. |
| PD-40 | Payout request policy | One effective request everywhere: `max(rulebook payout size (or the personal override), ladder.minRequestAmount ?? plan.minPayoutRequest)` via `effectivePayoutRequest(plan, requested)` in core. Engine runs use `PayoutRequestPolicy.FullRequestOnly`: the tracker pays only when it can pay the full effective request, unless a firm cap (ladder step, payout cap) makes the full request impossible, in which case that cap is the full amount. The engine's current behaviour (`UpToRequest`: pay `min(request, available)` down to the plan minimum, `FundedPayoutCycle.ts:417-435`) stays the default for existing callers and the CLI. A `PayoutPolicyDiffers` reason is added whenever an engine source's payout policy differs from the headline's (for example the payout-size sweep, or a DP row without PT-47). |
| PD-41 | Live model applicability | `LIVE_PLAN_BUILDERS` is keyed by firm, not plan (`firms/index.ts:51-62`; MFF's only builder is Rapid live). A typed per-plan table (`LivePlanApplicability`: plan serial to `{builder, verified}` or `NotModeled`), sourced from PT-04 research, decides which live builder models an account's live stage. Where only a firm-level builder exists, the advice, the live tool and the round-trip scope carry `LiveModelApproximation`. TopStep's `ReserveLivePlan` reserve progress (`LivePlan.ts:57-81`) is not stored; its live reconstruction uses the stored live start balance and disclosed reserve defaults with `LiveModelApproximation`, and is excluded from the round-trip claim. |
| PD-42 | Same-wave type coupling | Disjoint files are not enough. Before a wave starts, the orchestrator checks that no package in the wave adds a member to, removes from, or changes the shape of an enum, union or interface that another package in the same wave switches over, maps exhaustively (`Record<Enum, ...>`), constructs, or renders. Purely additive output fields that no other package in the wave reads are allowed and named in the wave log. Types a later package extends are declared in full by the package that introduces them (every `PayoutBlockReason`, `DifferenceReason`, `AssumptionKind` and `DailyPlanCard` field is declared up front; later packages change behaviour, not the type). |
| PD-43 | Tool catalog flags | Each `ToolId` entry carries `usesCalculatorInputs` (inputs summary, Edit dialog, URL writer) and `sharesState` (toolbar share link), plus an optional own URL codec. Simulator, analysis, sizing, compare, cash-flow, ladder-lab, strategy-lab, planner, funded-optimizer and live use calculator inputs and share them. Position size and payout planner use their own inputs, each with its own tested URL codec for the share link. The rules browser shares nothing. Anonymous public tools use `DEFAULT_RULEBOOK`; the signed-in rulebook query runs only when a session exists. |
| PD-44 | Personal caps only tighten (2026-10-02, default taken in PT-68d) | A trader's personal max risk per trade and personal daily profit cap cap every documented eval rung and the daily ceiling; they never raise a rung. With a personal cap set, the eval ladder's rungs can sum to less than the cushion (PD-14's last rung takes the remaining cushion only when no personal cap binds). Rationale: a personal cap is the trader's own risk limit and the advice must never size above it. Open for the user to overrule (question QP-1 in the waiting list). |

## Screens

Public, under `/prop-calculator`:
- **Hub** (`/`): tool cards grouped as Simulate, Analyse, Size, Compare, Plan, Labs and My accounts (one `<h1>`, one `<h2>` per group). Blurbs come from the tool catalog (it owns the missing ones; `panelDescriptions` is reused only where a key exists). Card hrefs append the last tool query from sessionStorage (F-38). A quick firm/plan start, recently used tools, and a signed-in accounts teaser (spend, payouts, net, active accounts, alert count) or a sign-in call to action. A legacy `#<section>` hash is forwarded to its tool page with the fragment kept.
- **Simulator:** the shared inputs form (firm/plan picker, plan badges, trading inputs), results, percentile bars, charts, "save as account".
- **Analysis:** strategy analysis (with Kelly), tail risk, drawdown duration, resilience, rule stress test.
- **Sizing:** optimal risk table, sensitivity heatmap.
- **Compare:** plan comparison, firm comparison, "open in simulator" per row (provider dispatch, PD-8).
- **Cash flow:** CashFlowPanel.
- **Ladder lab:** LadderLabPanel; the last run, its inputs and the display instrument survive navigation; a changed input shows "inputs changed since this run"; a run interrupted by navigation shows "search cancelled by navigation" on return.
- **Strategy lab.**
- **Multi-firm planner:** today's PortfolioPanel.
- **Position size (new):** risk $, instrument (NQ/MNQ/ES/MES), today's stop points, plan and phase. Shows contracts at that stop, the leftover dollars and the entry-offset stop that lands the risk exactly, the minimum stop points at the plan contract cap, and the cap itself. Own share link.
- **Funded optimizer (new):** the web `optimize funded`: the CLI's candidate set in the CLI's order and columns (including survivors), run in a worker under the `EnginePolicy` (retained cushion, effective payout request, lifetime cap), standard errors once PT-44 lands.
- **Live (new):** `simulateLiveAccount` for plans with a live builder, with `LiveModelApproximation` where only a firm-level builder exists. E8/FTMO show "no live stage modeled".
- **Rules (new):** the plan rules browser from one shared describe library.
- **Payout planner (new):** plan, balance, peak, payouts, last payout date in; readiness with the blocking gate, the rule-capped withdrawable (primary), net after split, and a size sweep out. Own share link.

Every tool page has exactly one `<h1>` (catalog label) followed by the toolbar where the catalog says it shares state, `<h2>` section headings, and the area subnav (base route plus the debounced query, `prefetch={false}`, `aria-current` on the most specific match, `aria-label`). Pages with `usesCalculatorInputs` also show the inputs summary and the edit dialog.

Private, under `/prop-calculator/accounts` (sign-in, noindex):
- **Overview:**
  - KPI row led by spend, payouts received and net (SKILL.md "track two numbers"), realized net cash per month per active slot with n and SE next to the modeled expected monthly net, then ROI; total nominal funding is secondary.
  - Alerts center, cushion board, payout readiness board (rule-capped withdrawable primary).
  - Exposure (first-trade risk and maximum daily loss per account and per group).
  - Expected monthly net per slot and total, two numbers per plan: under the documented policy (headline) and under the engine payout-size optimum, each labeled (fresh start, trials, retained cushion, payout policy, lifetime cap basis).
  - Plan cap usage and free slots (pooled caps once verified), funnel, diversification, cost analytics (refunds netted), realized vs modeled, replacement stats, monthly statement, timeline.
  - The accounts table: filters, sort, tags, archive.
- **New / Edit account:** firm, plan and variant with availability tags, only the opt-ins the plan offers, size, stage (Eval hidden for instant-funded plans), dates, label, alias, copy group, "replaces account", personal stricter rules, dashboard convention, live start balance for live accounts, and the initial snapshot. Fields are conditional on the drawdown kind; plausibility errors name the likely convention mix-up before anything is stored.
- **Account detail:**
  - plan rules summary, "plan rules changed since purchase" banner when the fingerprint differs;
  - reconstructed floor, lock and cushion with assumptions and the snapshot date;
  - the advice card (headline, engine optima, reasons, provenance with firm-data verification date), daily plan card (rungs capped to consistency and live-trigger ceilings, "stop for today"), payout advice;
  - DP advice if stored (with "not validated" unless a fresh gate run passed);
  - snapshot history chart; payouts, fees and events with forms; suggestion log;
  - live-transition preview (TopStep, Lucid); "simulate this account".
- **Weekly review:** bulk snapshot entry for all active accounts with plausibility checks, size changes vs last week, accept sizes.
- **Ledger:** all payouts and fees, CSV export.
- **Import:** CSV paste of accounts and snapshots, per-row validation (schema and plausibility) preview, commit.
- **Rulebook:** the documented-rule parameters, the deviation diff against the skill, alert thresholds, and import from the active trade-checklist plan with a diff preview.
- **Copy groups:** members (one stage), group sizing, exposure, later cross-plan group simulation.
- **Next slot:** which plan to buy next, by expected monthly net per slot within verified cap headroom, two numbers per plan (documented policy and engine payout-size optimum) with "payout-policy sensitive" marks, firms under live exclusivity or a cooldown excluded, firms with an unverified live trigger listed separately.
- **Edge:** journal expectancy and win rate vs the assumed 40% and 1:2, display only.

## Data model (`src/server/db/schemas/prop.ts`, `createTable` prefix `sadranl_`)

Common to every table:
- `id uuid pk defaultRandom`;
- `user_id text notNull references user.id on delete cascade` (accounting precedent, `schemas/accounting.ts:51-53`);
- `created_at`/`updated_at timestamptz default CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`;
- enums as `varchar(n).$type<Enum>()` with `import type`, no DB default (PD-24);
- dates as `varchar(10)` ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` (`accounting.ts:77-78`);
- money as `integer` cents;
- jsonb defaults as `sql` literals.

Row types are `typeof table.$inferSelect` aliases exported next to the tables. There are no hand-written interfaces.

| Table | Columns (besides the common ones) | Keys and indexes |
|---|---|---|
| `prop_account` | `firm_id` FirmId, `plan_serial` varchar(64), `account_size` integer, `opt_ins` jsonb PlanOptIns, `stage` AccountStage (Eval, Funded, Live), `status` AccountStatus (Active, Suspended, Busted, Concluded, Closed), `label` varchar(64), `external_alias` varchar(64) null, `purchased_on`, `funded_on` null, `first_funded_trade_on` null, `live_start_balance_cents` null (the live starting or transition balance, for live reconstruction), `dashboard_convention` DashboardBalanceConvention, `copy_group_id` uuid null (FK set null), `replaces_account_id` uuid null (self FK set null), `personal_rules` jsonb PersonalRules default `'{}'`, `tags` jsonb string[] default `'[]'` (<= 20 x 32 chars), `notes` text null (<= 2,000), `plan_rules_fingerprint` varchar(64) null (filled from PT-45), `archived_at` timestamptz null | table `unique('prop_account_id_user_id_uq').on(id, user_id)`; unique index `(user_id, label) where archived_at is null`; index `(user_id, status)`, `(user_id, firm_id)` |
| `prop_account_snapshot` (append-only) | `account_id`, `as_of`, `balance_cents`, `highest_eod_balance_cents` null, `highest_intraday_balance_cents` null, `payouts_taken` null, `trading_days` null, `qualifying_days_since_last_payout` null, `balance_at_last_payout_cents` null, `floor_at_last_payout_cents` null, `last_payout_on` null, `cycle_best_day_profit_cents` null, `eval_best_day_profit_cents` null, `cumulative_payout_cents` null (trader-received net, PD-15), `last_traded_on` null, `dashboard_floor_cents` null, `source` SnapshotSource (Manual, WeeklyReview, Import) | composite FK `(account_id, user_id)` to `prop_account(id, user_id)` cascade; index `(user_id, account_id, as_of)` |
| `prop_payout` | `account_id`, `requested_on`, `paid_on` null, `gross_cents`, `net_cents` null, `status` PayoutStatus (Requested, Paid, Denied, Cancelled), `note` null | composite FK; index `(user_id, account_id)`, `(user_id, paid_on)` |
| `prop_fee` | `account_id`, `kind` FeeKind (EvalPurchase, Reset, Rebuy, Activation, Subscription, FundedReset, Refund, Other), `amount_cents` (non-negative; Refund subtracts), `paid_on`, `note` null | composite FK; index `(user_id, paid_on)` |
| `prop_account_event` | `account_id`, `kind` AccountEventKind (Purchased, EvalPassed, Busted, FundedReset, Concluded, MovedLive, ClosedInactivity, Refunded, Closed, Reopened, Suspended, Resumed, Edited), `occurred_on`, `detail` jsonb AccountEventDetail (bounded) | composite FK; index `(user_id, account_id, occurred_on)`, `(user_id, occurred_on)` |
| `prop_copy_group` | `name` varchar(64), `notes` null | table `unique().on(id, user_id)`; unique `(user_id, name)` |
| `prop_rulebook` | `user_id` primary key (liftingSettings precedent, `schemas/lifting.ts:367-397`), `parameters` jsonb RulebookParameters (with `schemaVersion`; absent row = `DEFAULT_RULEBOOK`) | pk `user_id` |
| `prop_sizing_decision` | `account_id`, `snapshot_id` uuid null (FK set null, ownership-checked), `decided_on`, `stage`, `headline_risk_cents`, `accepted_risk_cents`, `accepted_rungs_cents` jsonb int[] (<= 20), `source` AdviceSource, `actual_risk_cents` null, `note` null | composite FK; index `(user_id, account_id, decided_on)` |
| `prop_saved_scenario` | `name` varchar(64), `query` text (<= 8 KB) | unique `(user_id, name)` |
| `prop_dp_advice` (PT-30, migration 0008) | `account_id`, `snapshot_id` (ownership-checked), `plan_serial`, `plan_rules_fingerprint`, `config_key` varchar(64) (sha256 of every honored solve parameter), `config` jsonb, `objective` varchar, `solver_version` integer, `solved_at`, `eligible` boolean, `ineligible_reason` varchar null, `validated` boolean, `validation_ref` varchar null (ledger file and row), `samples` jsonb DpAdviceSamples, `gaps` jsonb, `runtime_ms` integer | composite FK; index `(user_id, account_id, solved_at)` |

## API (`propAccounts` router in `src/server/api/root.ts`, nested like `routers/lifting/index.ts`)

All procedures use `protectedProcedure`, with Zod `.input()` and `.output()`.
- Every select, update and delete on `sadranl_prop_*` has `"user_id" = <session userId>` in its WHERE clause, including inside `tx` (ESLint does not cover `tx`, `eslint.config.js:74-81`).
- Child writes first call `loadOwnedAccountOrThrow(accountId, userId)` (NOT_FOUND on miss); every `snapshot_id`, `copy_group_id` and `replaces_account_id` is ownership-checked the same way on every path that sets it (create, update, importMany rows, copyGroup.assign, decision.create), and a snapshot must belong to the decision's account (`id`, `user_id` and `account_id` all match). `copyGroup.assign`'s mixed-stage check runs on create, update and importMany too. Removes clear the referencing columns first in the same transaction (PD-20). A Postgres unique violation on the active label (23505) maps to CONFLICT with a readable message, never a 500.
- Mutations pass the rate limit and per-user row caps (PD-36).

| Sub-router | Procedures |
|---|---|
| `account` | `list({includeArchived, stage?, firmId?})`, `get({id})`, `create` (stage validated against the plan, PD-29), `update` (stage and status excluded from the schema; writes an `Edited` event with a field diff in the same transaction), `archive`, `unarchive`, `remove`, `importMany` (<= 200 rows, one transaction, row caps checked) |
| `snapshot` | `listForAccount`, `latestForAll`, `create`, `bulkCreate` (<= 200), `remove` (records an `Edited` event) |
| `payout` | `list({accountId?})`, `create`, `update`, `remove` |
| `fee` | `list({accountId?})`, `create`, `update`, `remove` |
| `event` | `listForAccount`, `list({from?, to?, accountId?})` (userId-scoped, range at most 3 years, at most 5,000 rows, ordered by `occurred_on`), `record` (validated by the plan-aware `AccountLifecycle`; updates stage/status in the same transaction, both scoped) |
| `copyGroup` | `list`, `create`, `update`, `remove`, `assign({accountId, copyGroupId or null})` (both ids ownership-checked; mixed stage rejected) |
| `rulebook` | `get` (defaults when no row; parsed with defaulting schema), `upsert`, `reset` |
| `decision` | `listForAccount`, `create`, `recordActual` |
| `scenario` | `list`, `save`, `remove` |
| `dpAdvice` (PT-30) | `listForAccount`, `latestForAll` (read-only; rows are written by the CLI) |
| `edge` (PT-39) | `summary({from?, to?})` over `tradeAssessments` scoped by userId |

Overview metrics are computed client-side from the lists (accounts, latest snapshots, payouts, fees, `event.list`) by pure `~/lib/prop-accounts/metrics` functions. Simulation-backed numbers run in Web Workers.

## Suggester design (part C)

The code lives in `src/lib/prop-calculator/advisor/` (browser-safe barrel `~/lib/prop-calculator/advisor`). DP code lives in `advisor/dp/` (Node-only, never re-exported, PD-16). An import-graph test proves the barrel never reaches `FundedStateValue.ts`, `AverageRewardSolver.ts` or any `node:` module.

**Documented rules.** `DocumentedRule` is the abstract base. Subclasses: `EvalLadderRule` (default, general derivation; MFF search fractions opt-in), `EvalMaxRiskRule` (opt-in), `FundedFixedRiskRule`, `LiveCushionPercentRule`, `PayoutRequestRule` (PT-46).
- Input `RuleContext`: cushion, day-start DLL room (or none), personal DLL (or none), consistency daily cap (or none), profit target and remaining profit to target (eval), rr, lock state, the plan's contract limit and instrument for the minimum-stop report, the live cushion percent resolved by the plan's applicable `LivePlan` (PD-41, built with the rulebook's `LiveCushionPercent`, then `cushionPercentFor`), and for payouts the reconstructed tracker, `evaluatePayout` result and effective request. Caps arrive as inputs computed by `resolveRiskAt` (PT-13) and the plan's own methods; rules never re-derive `plan.affordableRisk`.
- Output `DocumentedSizing`: rungs, TP per rung, daily profit cap, `DayStopRule`, max trades, running-loss column, `minStopPointsAtCap`, notes and `RuleSource[]` (section anchors). The funded rule also states its trades per day and stop rule, which `EnginePolicy` carries.
- Live risk goes through `resolveLiveTradeRisk` (`core/LiveSizing.ts:20`); E8/FTMO (no live builder) select the rulebook percent by the reconstructed lock flag and call the same function.
- They are pure and instant. `DocumentedPolicySpec` plus `toSimInputs` (PT-48, PD-39) make the same rules runnable by `simulate`.

**Reconstruction.** `AccountReconstruction.rebuild(snapshotInput, plan)` returns a `ReconstructedAccount`: plan with opt-ins, phase, full `AccountState` (including peak day-close profit, peak intraday profit, intraday high, best day, qualifying days, consecutive idle days, elapsed days), `FundedCycleTracker` (including `fundedResetsUsed` in its `cycleSnapshot`) or live state, floor, lock, cushion, resolved DLL and contract limit, `Assumption[]` and warnings. The steps:
1. Validate plausibility (F-140): a pre-lock cushion above the plan's drawdown on a trailing plan, a nominal balance far from the account size, or a $0-based balance near the account size fail loud with the likely convention error.
2. Start from `plan.initialState()` or `plan.beginFundedPhase` (`Plan.ts:723-728`, `636-650`; re-read after G2).
3. Replay the peak through the plan's own `DrawdownStrategy`. For lock-effect plans use the floor at last payout when given; otherwise take the conservative order and disclose it.
4. Apply `payoutFloorEffect` for payouts already taken.
5. Seed the tracker's public fields (payouts issued, cumulative payout as trader-received net, `fundedResetsUsed` from events) and calendar-gate progress from real dates.
6. Cross-check a given dashboard floor: keep the higher floor and warn on mismatch.
7. Build the live stage from the plan's applicable live builder (PD-41; `LiveModelApproximation` where only a firm-level builder exists; TopStep reserve state from the live start balance plus disclosed defaults); E8/FTMO "not modeled", the documented live rule still works from the dashboard floor.

**Payout readiness.** A core enum `PayoutGate` (`core/PayoutGate.ts`, exported from `core/index.ts`) has one member per null-returning branch of `tryPayout` (including early withdrawal and `resolveWithdrawal`'s ladder, min-request and deny-if-unaffordable checks), plus `AccountConcluded` (`Plan.isAccountConcluded`, lifetime payout count, `Plan.ts:811-819`) and `LadderExhausted` (closeout-credit ladder). The new non-mutating `FundedCycleTracker.evaluatePayout` (extracted from `tryPayout`) returns a `PayoutGate` or eligible plus amount. The advisor-level `PayoutBlockReason` (`advisor/PayoutBlockReason.ts`) wraps it as a union: `{kind: Gate, gate}`, `{kind: PayoutPending}` (an open Requested payout), `{kind: WouldTriggerLive, trigger}` (declared in PT-12, returned only once PT-36 supplies verified triggers). Core never imports advisor. `PayoutReadiness` builds on both.

**Advisor.** `SizingAdvisor` is the abstract base with `EvalSizingAdvisor`, `FundedSizingAdvisor` and `LiveSizingAdvisor`, built by `createSizingAdvisor(stage, plan)` (instant-funded plans reject Eval). API:
- `documented()`: sync; stale snapshots per PD-28 return `StaleAdvice` with no amounts.
- `caps()`: `RiskCaps` from `resolveRiskAt` plus personal caps.
- `optimumRequests()`: serializable `EngineOptimumRequest[]`, each carrying the full `EnginePolicy` (PD-26).
- `assemble(results)`: an `Advice` holding headline (with custom-rule label), optima, `DifferenceReason[]`, assumptions (including "live triggers not checked" until PT-36), `Provenance`, `DailyPlanCard` and `PayoutAdvice`.

**Daily plan card.** Rungs and TP with running loss, daily cap, stop conditions and one trade per window (Hard Rule 6). When a ceiling applies (the funded consistency maximum before the payout is pushed out, or a verified single-day live trigger from PT-36), every rung is capped at `(ceiling - running PnL) / rr` after the documented and DLL caps, and the card shows "stop for today" once that cap falls below the placeable minimum (one micro contract at the entered stop, else $1). It never suggests an early exit (Hard Rule 1).

**Engine optimum sources (`AdviceSource`)**, each with its objective and start basis:

| Source | Engine call | Where it runs | Start basis | Package |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| LadderSearchFresh | `runLadderSearch` bySpeed, bounded grid | browser worker | fresh eval | PT-19 |
| LadderSearchFromState | `runLadderSearch` with `startState`: current attempt scored from state, retries priced at fresh-start scores, day cap minus elapsed days | browser worker | current eval state | PT-15, PT-19 |
| LedgerRecordedLadder | the ledger's per-plan fastest ladder (typed table citing the ledger file and row, stale-checked against the ledger index) | static | fresh eval (historical) | PT-19 |
| FundedSweepFresh | lifted CLI candidates, `simulate()` per candidate under the `EnginePolicy`, ranked by `expectedMonthlyNet` as the CLI does | browser worker | fresh funded | PT-16, PT-19 |
| FundedSweepFromState | same candidates, `SimInputs.start`, ranked by the from-state objective (PT-14) | browser worker | current funded state | PT-14, PT-32 |
| PayoutSizeSweep | `payoutRequestSize` sweep (each size effective and FullRequestOnly) | browser worker | fresh, then from-state | PT-32 |
| DpAtState | funded average-reward DP / eval DP at the rebuilt state, with the retained cushion and (after PT-47) the payout request policy threaded into the solve | CLI only, stored | exact state | PT-18, PT-47, PT-30 |

**DP strategy (PD-16).**
1. One solve per distinct full config: every parameter the solver honors (plan serial, opt-ins, plan-rule fingerprint, winrate, rr, tpd, commission, grid, rebuy lag days, funded-days hazard, eval days, fees and discounts, day-stop rule, cushion-step multiple, retained cushion, and after PT-47 the effective payout request and policy) is hashed into `config_key`. `prop advise --dp` passes `fundedGrid.minRetainedCushion` from the rulebook (today `optimize dp` passes no `fundedGrid` at all).
2. Eligibility via `isFundedDpEligible` and `isEvalDpEligible`, computed in the CLI and stored with the reason.
3. Model gaps from `fundedDpModelGaps`, plus a state-fit report for grid saturation, plus `PayoutSizeNotModeled` when PT-47 is not in place. `DpGridMisaligned` (eval drawdown not a multiple of `cushionStepDollars`) is informational only: the skill calls an even divisor "necessary-looking but not sufficient", and AlphaFutures Advanced ($1,750 drawdown, $200 step) was measured ADOPT.
4. Eval: reached-state query (PT-18); elapsed sessions mapped explicitly to the DP day index; states past the solve's eval-days horizon give `DpStateUnreached`; the funded `computeRisk` throw for unreachable levels is caught into a typed reason.
5. Objective shown in provenance. Eval DP rows appear only for plans with a current eval-DP validation under the cash objective (terminal value at pass tied to the funded continuation value) recorded in the ledger; otherwise they are suppressed with `DpNotValidated`, and a pass-probability objective is suppressed with `DpObjectiveMismatch` (every eval-DP verdict in the skill is historical, pass-probability).
6. `validated` is true only from a post-G2 gate run recorded in the ledger with its engine commit, a retained cushion matched between the DP and the flat baseline (PD-26), and DP >= the best flat policy by the gate; otherwise "not validated" with the gate result named. The 2026-09-23 validation-2 file does not qualify (stale tree on 65e310c; TopStep ran at a $0 retained cushion against a $2,000 flat baseline and scored 0.99x). PT-30 re-runs the gate and appends the ledger row.
7. Staleness: newer snapshot, different `solver_version`, or different plan-rule fingerprint. A pinned toy-solve characterization test forces a `DP_ADVICE_SOLVER_VERSION` bump.
8. A per-run plan cap and time budget with resumable per-plan output; runtime printed.
9. Verified conduct clauses against aggressive day-1 sizing (for example TopStep FTP Slowdown / RTP, FTMO inconsistent position sizing, if PT-04 confirms them) attach `AggressiveOptimumChurn` with the firm's quote to DP rows and sweep rows alike.

**Reasons (`DifferenceReason`, text from typed fields, all declared in PT-19):** ObjectiveSpeedVsMonthlyNet, FreshStartApproximation, AggressiveOptimumChurn (only from a verified conduct pattern, with the quote), WithinNoise (only where SEs exist, PD-34), CushionCap, DailyLossCap, ConsistencyCap, RemainingTargetCap, CeilingCap, PersonalCap, RetainedCushionBasis, FirmMinimumAboveRequest, PayoutPolicyDiffers, DpIneligible, DpModelGap, DpGridSaturation, DpGridMisaligned (informational), DpStateUnreached, DpObjectiveMismatch, DpNotValidated, DpPayoutPolicyMismatch, ConsistencyNotEvaluated, AssumedInputs, StaleAdvice, LiveNotModeled, LiveModelApproximation, LiveTriggersNotChecked, WouldTriggerLive, PlanRulesChanged.

## Page split design (part A)

Route tree:

```
src/app/(app)/prop-calculator/
  page.tsx                       hub (public, dynamic like every route; renders its own <main>)
  (tools)/layout.tsx             server layout: client CalculatorProvider, subnav, <main> around children
  (tools)/simulator|analysis|sizing|compare|cash-flow|ladder-lab|strategy-lab|planner/page.tsx
  (tools)/position-size|funded-optimizer|live|rules|payout-planner/page.tsx
  accounts/layout.tsx            session guard, noindex, subnav
  accounts/... (overview, new, [id], [id]/edit, review, ledger, import, rulebook, copy-groups, next-slot, edge)
  _components/ (unchanged location: 19 tests import it, knip.json:7 entry, worker URL in useLadderSearch.ts:268)
  _workers/
```

- **State:** `CalculatorProvider` splits four contexts: inputs state (reducer state and firm memory), base result plus pending flags, lab slots (pinned, chartType, ladder slot), and memoized actions (created once over `dispatch`). The computation cache lives in a ref, never in React state, so writes never re-render consumers. The ladder slot is written only on completion, cancel or unmount. The reducer is initialized lazily from `useSearchParams()` (PD-8 a); the legacy hash is handled in a mount effect before the first URL write; one writer on `[state, pathname, searchParams]`.
- **Computation cache:** namespaced by a `ComputationId` enum (BaseSimulation, RuleStress, OptimalRisk, Sensitivity, PlanComparison, FirmComparison, CashFlow, Planner, StrategyLab), typed through a `ComputationResultMap` so `get`/`set<Id>(id, key)` return that id's result type. A pure `initialFor(id, key, cache)` returns `{result, pending, shouldCompute}` and drives `useDebouncedComputation`'s `useState` initializers and an early return in its mount effect, so a cache hit neither flashes nor recomputes.
- **Navigation:** `RouteSubnav` items are keyed by base route, carry the debounced query with `prefetch={false}` (PD-8 d), and the active item is resolved across the whole list by `activeSubnavHref(pathname, items)` (most specific match wins, so a Prefix parent never shares `aria-current="page"` with an exact child); the `<nav>` gets an `aria-label`. `SectionNav` is retired (both render into `#navbar-subnav-slot`, `Navbar.tsx:67`).
- **Hub round trip:** the hub is outside the provider. The last encoded query is kept in sessionStorage (try/catch) and appended to hub card hrefs, so inputs survive; pinned scenarios and lab results reset on leaving the tools area, and the hub says so.
- **Loading:** each page imports only its panels. Heavy panels load through `next/dynamic` with `Skeleton` fallbacks. Result-consuming sections render a `Skeleton` until the base result is non-null (PD-10).
- **SEO, headings and landmarks:** each page exports `metadata` (title, description, canonical without query) via `buildToolMetadata(ToolId)`. The layout owns `<main>`; `ToolPageHeading` renders the `<h1>` then the toolbar; each section is a `<section aria-labelledby>` with an `<h2>` (PD-7). Tool pages go in `indexableRoutes`, accounts in `disallowedCrawlPaths` (`routes.ts:68-88`). The stale "6 firms" description (`page.tsx:9`) is fixed from `ALL_FIRMS` (10).

Section id to page (single table for the legacy fragment map, the page shells and the trace; each of the 15 sections appears on exactly one page, and the shell keeps its id):

| Legacy section ids | Page |
|---|---|
| simulator, charts (toolbar moves to `ToolPageHeading`) | /simulator |
| strategy, tail-risk, drawdown, resilience, rule-stress-test | /analysis |
| optimal-risk, sensitivity | /sizing |
| plan-comparison, firm-comparison | /compare |
| cash-flow | /cash-flow |
| ladder-lab-section, ladder-lab (inner) | /ladder-lab |
| strategy-lab | /strategy-lab |
| portfolio | /planner |

## Schedule

Four lanes. Packages in one wave own disjoint files; shared barrels are owned by exactly one package per wave. Waves of different lanes may run concurrently only because their file sets are disjoint by lane: lane W owns `src/app/**`, `src/server/**`, `src/proxy.ts`, `src/lib/site/**`, `src/lib/schemas/**` and `src/lib/prop-accounts/{csv,edge}/**`; lane E owns `src/lib/prop-calculator/**`, `src/cli/**`, `src/lib/prop-accounts/{advice,metrics,alerts}/**` and `_components/simInputsCacheKey.ts` (PT-14). Before starting any wave the orchestrator (1) diffs its owned files against every in-flight wave and stops on overlap, and (2) applies PD-42: no package in the wave may change an enum, union or interface that another package in the same wave switches over, maps exhaustively, constructs or renders.

- **Lane 0 (now, parallel with audit wave 19): waves 1 and 2.** New files plus `server/db/index.ts`, `server/api/root.ts`, `core/TradingFirm.ts` (method only; re-read before editing; PT-01 first checks the audit tracker and `designs.md` for items fingerprinting it and records the handoff) and `RouteSubnav.tsx` (done). No file under `src/app/(app)/prop-calculator/**`, no WP24 file (`labMath.ts`, the root and firms barrels, `sim/command.ts`, `optimize/dp/command.ts`, the engine files listed under "Sibling workstream", the three label tests), no `core/index.ts`. Before wave 1, record a baseline of `bun run typecheck` and `bunx knip` output; a lane-0 wave fails only on new errors in its own files or files importing them. RED uses typed stubs (PD-22) so a project-wide typecheck stays at the baseline, and no lane 0 package is between RED and GREEN while an audit package or audit check runs (U21).
- **G1:** WP24 implemented and re-reviewed (APPROVE), and the audit's santa pass has recorded sha256 fingerprints for every audit item whose files lie in `src/app/(app)/prop-calculator/**` or `src/lib/site/**`; U21 agreed. Re-read every lane-W owned file; record the audit outcome and the fingerprinted items lane W will touch in the wave log and the handoff row.
- **Lane W (after G1):** W3 PT-10; W4 PT-11, PT-17, PT-20; W5 PT-43, PT-28, PT-29, PT-39, PT-40; W6 PT-41. After every W wave the still-mounted shell is traced to keep working until PT-43 deletes it.
- **G2:** the audit's final santa pass over every item, its fingerprint re-check, its engine-results rerun (it drives the CLI on this tree, so no engine edit may land while it runs; added 2026-09-26) and its `bun run check`, with outcomes recorded. Not included: the parked firm-data steps waiting on pasted pages and any T4 fee-sweep rows blocked on 403 pages; these continue in parallel, and every lane E package that edits a firm file re-checks the audit tracker for such rows first and stops if one is in flight. Capture characterization pins from the working tree at the start of each lane E package (PD-31).
- **Lane E (after G2):** E1 PT-13, PT-15, PT-16, PT-18; E2 PT-12; E3 PT-14, PT-46, PT-35; E4 PT-47, PT-48, PT-22, PT-44 (PT-44's `SimOutputs` change is additive and read by no other E4 package; named in the wave log); E5 PT-19; E6 PT-32; E7 PT-24. `core/index.ts` is owned by PT-13 in E1, PT-12 in E2 and PT-35 in E3; `advisor/index.ts` by PT-12 in E2, PT-46 in E3, PT-19 in E5, PT-32 in E6 (PT-48 has its own `advisor/policy/index.ts`, re-exported by PT-19); `simulator/index.ts` by PT-14 in E3 and PT-44 in E4.
- **Lane J (after lane E is complete and the named W packages are implemented):** J1 PT-21, PT-23, PT-26, PT-31, PT-49; J2 PT-25, PT-27, PT-33, PT-45; J3 PT-36; J4 PT-34, PT-37, PT-38, PT-42; J5 PT-30. J waves run alone (no concurrent W or E wave) unless the orchestrator's file diff and PD-42 check are empty.
- **Firm files (PT-35):** only after G2; re-check the audit tracker's firm-data rows first.
- **Wave checks:** `bun run typecheck` against the baseline; `bunx eslint <every file the wave touched>` (no `--fix`); the formatter on owned files only; `bunx vitest run` on every test file the wave touched plus `tests/unit/app/prop-calculator` and the guard tests named in each package; importer greps for new and deleted modules; `bunx knip` (reported, weak evidence per PD-22). The full 13-minute suite is not a wave gate; it runs once in PT-50 if the user wants it.

## Review plan

| Change type | Reviewers (ECC) |
|---|---|
| Every package | `code-reviewer`, `typescript-reviewer` |
| Any `.tsx` | `react-reviewer` |
| Routers, repo, proxy/auth, CSV import, CLI DB writes (PT-01, PT-05, PT-20, PT-28, PT-30, PT-39, PT-40, PT-43, PT-49) | `security-reviewer` |
| Schema, migrations, queries, transactions (PT-01, PT-05, PT-30, PT-39) | `database-reviewer` |
| Sizing and payout logic (PT-03, PT-12, PT-19, PT-22, PT-26, PT-32, PT-35, PT-36, PT-38, PT-46, PT-47, PT-48) | a trader-rules reviewer checking every output against SKILL.md Hard Rules 1-8 |
| Engine changes (PT-12 to PT-16, PT-18, PT-30, PT-35, PT-42, PT-44, PT-47, PT-48) | plus a characterization check against pins captured from the working tree |

The fix loop runs until no CRITICAL or HIGH findings remain; MEDIUM findings are fixed or handed off with the receiving package named.

## Risks and mitigations

- **Collision with the audit.** G1 (WP24 done, web/site fingerprints recorded) and G2 (final santa pass, fingerprint re-check, `bun run check`), lanes with disjoint files, fingerprint handoffs, U21 on repo-wide rewriting commands and RED windows, typed-stub RED, typecheck baseline, re-read before every edit, never revert another package's hunks.
- **Wrong sizing or withdrawal on real money.** Documented rules follow Hard Rules 2-6 with cumulative DLL, consistency caps, the remaining-target cap and post-payout floors; the general-derivation ladder by default; ceilings sized into the rungs; the effective payout request respects plan minimums; engine runs always use the user's retained cushion, the full-request payout policy and the verified live-trigger cap; plausibility checks at every entry point; per-stage staleness; instant-funded lifecycle; same-stage copy groups; round-trip tests for every drawdown subclass and every modeled live pair; trader-rules reviewer.
- **Engine numbers under a different policy than the headline.** `EnginePolicy` everywhere, `PayoutPolicyDiffers`, two numbers per plan with sensitivity marks, DP payout policy in the solver (PT-47) or a typed gap.
- **Cross-user data leak.** Composite FKs with in-table unique targets, ownership checks on every reference, WHERE-clause assertions per procedure including `tx` and `event.list`, abuse limits, security review on every router package.
- **Engine optimum presented as current when stale or fresh-start only.** Provenance on every number, start basis, snapshot date, plan-rule fingerprint, StaleAdvice and FreshStartApproximation reasons; DP `validated` only from a fresh gate run.
- **Browser jank.** Workers for every sweep; base simulation after mount only, shared, from the lazily initialized state; four provider contexts; typed computation cache in a ref; bounded grids and trials.
- **DP runtime and memory.** CLI only, one solve per full config, eligibility gates, per-run budget, printed runtime.
- **Long lane E chain (7 waves).** Each wave is small and mergeable; E1 packages have no inter-dependencies; PD-42 prevents merge-time type breaks.
- **Migration drift.** Inspect generated SQL; stop on anything outside `sadranl_prop_*` or an FK before its target; the user decides when to migrate (U3).
- **Share-link regression.** `urlState.test.ts` unchanged; provider-based share link; URL sync table test including query-only navigations and hashes; legacy query and fragment table tests; fragment retention across the 307 confirmed in the Next 16 docs in PT-10, with the hub and provider mappers as the fallback path.
- **Next 16 specifics.** PT-10 confirms in the docs: proxy redirect semantics, route-group layout persistence, `usePathname` and `useSearchParams` on Back/Forward and after `history.replaceState`, `useSearchParams` in a client component under a dynamic layout (no Suspense bailout), `next/dynamic` in client components, Link `prefetch={false}` behaviour.
- **Firm data drift.** Nothing firm-specific without a live fetch; unverified badges; plan-rule fingerprints on stored advice.
- **Scope size.** Numbered items, each with a package; the findings tables; the wave log updated after every run.
